**[P0] Import + verify official roster: Montana (MT)**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-15, part of the Phase-1 backlog-population pass that brings every remaining jurisdiction up to build-ready status, per Muxin's decision to keep expanding the manual vertical-slice track (proven for AZ, TX, OK, AL) in parallel rather than one state at a time. No Wave-2/3 rehearsal (F03/I06/I11) covered Montana — this build's own research must first establish the official election authority's site, format, and whether it runs Civix-vended software (check the URL pattern against `docs/operations/nationwide-congressional-roster-plan.md`'s "Civix portal operational playbook" before assuming either way), exactly as AZ, TX, and OK each did from a cold start.
- OUTCOME: One more jurisdiction built and verified end-to-end through the pattern the AZ, TX, OK, and AL cards proved: official roster governs the candidate set + incumbent-on-ballot status for both US House — all MT districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists, flag-gated, zero behavior change elsewhere, verified live against an isolated Neon staging branch before merge.
- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm Montana's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Montana's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (both US House — all MT districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/mt-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/montana-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than Montana; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Montana source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Montana turns out to run Civix).
- STATUS: Done — code merged to main 2026-07-16 (PR #352 merged)
- STATUS: Backlog
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new jurisdiction's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; no pre-existing rehearsal data for this jurisdiction — the build's own research establishes the source from scratch, same as AZ/TX/OK originally — 2026-07-15

- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official New Mexico source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if New Mexico turns out to run Civix).

- STATUS: Backlog
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new jurisdiction's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.

- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm Oregon's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Oregon's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (both US House — all OR districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/or-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/oregon-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than Oregon; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.

- OUTCOME: One more jurisdiction built and verified end-to-end through the pattern the AZ, TX, OK, and AL cards proved: official roster governs the candidate set + incumbent-on-ballot status for both US House — all WA districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists, flag-gated, zero behavior change elsewhere, verified live against an isolated Neon staging branch before merge.
- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm Washington's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Washington's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (both US House — all WA districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/wa-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/washington-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than Washington; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Washington source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Washington turns out to run Civix).

- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; no pre-existing rehearsal data for this jurisdiction — the build's own research establishes the source from scratch, same as AZ/TX/OK originally — 2026-07-15

**[P2] Re-check official roster: Wyoming (WY) — after primary certification**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-16, opened per the epic's NOT-BEFORE date-gate convention as part of finishing the WY vertical-slice build. WY's fixture (`scripts/congressional-rosters/wy-official-roster-2026.ts`, `docs/operations/wyoming-vertical-slice-data-check.md`) recorded EVERY row (12 House + 7 Senate) as `qualified_for_primary_ballot` because Wyoming's Aug 18, 2026 primary had not yet happened at build time. Both WY federal seats are open in 2026 (Sen. Lummis retiring; Rep. Hageman filed for the open Senate seat instead of House re-election) — see the data-check doc for the full incumbency finding.
- OUTCOME: WY's fixture is updated with each party's certified nominee per office, once the primary is certified.
- IN SCOPE: re-verify against Wyoming's official primary results (sos.wyo.gov's 2026 Election Information page — check whether a results page or a post-primary roster PDF is published); replace each seat's `qualified_for_primary_ballot` rows with the certified nominee at `qualified_for_general_ballot` per party (Wyoming has no runoff — a plurality primary decides each party's nominee outright, so this is a straight promotion, not a `runoff_pending` derivation); re-run the importer against staging; update the deliverable doc; also check for any post-primary nominee vacancy/withdrawal per W.S. 22-5-401 (major party) / 22-5-403 (minor/provisional party) — see the data-check doc's Known gaps section, this has no single fixed statutory cutoff date, so treat it as an ongoing check, not a one-time box to tick.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent.
- SAFETY: same as the original WY card — official-source reads only, never guess a still-undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently, never infer a nominee from uncertified election-night numbers.
- NOT BEFORE: 2026-08-26 — Wyoming's Primary State Canvassing Board date (W.S. 22-16-118), the point the Aug 18 primary results become official/certified. Re-running right after the primary itself would risk locking in an uncertified result if any race is close enough to shift during the Aug 21 county canvass or an Aug 24 recount request (source: `wyoleg.gov/InterimCommittee/2025/07-202505084-032026DRAFTElectionCalendar.pdf`, confirmed 2026-07-16).
- STATUS: Backlog
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; WY's fixture reflects the certified general-ballot nominee(s) per office; end-to-end verification against staging; deliverable doc updated.
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate, same authorized scope as the original WY card.
- GROOMED: ready: certification date directly confirmed via Wyoming's own statutory election calendar — 2026-07-16

- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm U.S. Virgin Islands's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe U.S. Virgin Islands's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (the VI non-voting Delegate seat) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/vi-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/us-virgin-islands-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than U.S. Virgin Islands; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official U.S. Virgin Islands source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if U.S. Virgin Islands turns out to run Civix).
- BLOCKED (Muxin, 2026-07-15): shares the non-voting-territory gap found while scoping American Samoa (AS) — see `docs/operations/non-voting-territory-delegate-rendering-gap.md` and the new "[P1] DECISION NEEDED: non-voting-territory delegate rendering" card (parent `c5a813bb`) this card is now blocked on. Do not claim/build until that decision lands.
- STATUS: Backlog
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new jurisdiction's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; I11 rehearsal already inventoried this jurisdiction's source — 2026-07-15

**[P0] I12 — National inventory consolidation and semantic gate**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: Wave 3 fan-in of the nationwide official-source congressional roster plan (docs/operations/nationwide-congressional-roster-plan.md, "I12 — National inventory consolidation and semantic gate"); pre-created 2026-07-14 to carry the unattended run from Wave 3 into Wave 4.
- PRECONDITION (fan-in): ALL of I05, I06, I07, I08, I09, I10, I11 must be Done before this card starts. The board parser honors only ONE DEPENDS ON per card (first-wins), so this card names I11 (last roster-a group) but its TRUE blocker is all seven I-groups. Confirm every I05–I11 is Done on the Step-0.5 readiness re-check before starting.
- OUTCOME: One consolidated national inventory proving all 56 jurisdictions are accounted for; every expected 2026 contest maps to an official-source path or evidenced explicit state; parser families and access constraints frozen; non-sensitive public golden addresses selected for later app testing; no placeholder/unknown adapter promoted.
- IN SCOPE: Fan-in of the seven I-group inventories into one validated national record; cross-group reconciliation against the F02 exact-contest oracle; freeze of parser-family classes and access constraints; selection of public golden addresses; EMIT the exact pilot and adapter cards (P16 Texas, P17 Alabama, P18 California, and one P19 card per still-unproven source/semantic class) as new Backlog cards — each born decided (stamp its DECISION from the plan's "Pre-authorized execution decisions" pilots clause: official-source reads only per the NON-NEGOTIABLE SOURCE DECISION, writes only to isolated staging, fail-closed) and pre-GROOMED so the run continues without a drip-feed approval.
- OUT OF SCOPE: Candidate ingestion, schema migration (M13), promotion engine (M14), staging provisioning (M15), the pilots themselves, production mutation, scheduled refreshes.
- SAFETY: Fail-closed if any I-group is incomplete or any jurisdiction lacks an official-source path/evidenced state — never consolidate a partial national inventory; no placeholder/unknown adapter card may be promoted; a filing list or calendar-only source can never be recorded as a qualified/certified roster.

- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: Wave 4 of the nationwide official-source congressional roster plan (docs/operations/nationwide-congressional-roster-plan.md, "M14 — Private artifact abstraction and fail-closed promotion engine"); pre-groomed 2026-07-14.
- OUTCOME: A private-artifact storage abstraction and a fail-closed promotion engine proven, by TDD against fake Blob/database implementations, to retain the prior promoted snapshot in every failure case.

- TESTS: A TDD suite drives the engine entirely through fake Blob/database implementations; every enumerated failure case asserts prior-snapshot retention and no promotion.

- GROOMED: ready: explicit fake-only TDD scope, fail-closed retention safeguard, and full-failure-case goal condition — 2026-07-14

**[P2] Build official roster: Louisiana (LA) US House — after Nov 3 open-primary qualifying closes**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-15, opened as part of Louisiana's own vertical-slice build per the epic's "NOT BEFORE DATE-GATE CONVENTION" standing requirement. Unlike every prior state's re-check card (a primary-stage roster waiting on certification to promote to general-ballot nominees), this one is a genuine BUILD, not a promotion: Louisiana's US House races use a new-for-2026 open ("jungle") primary on Nov 3, 2026 (Act 7 of the 2026 Regular Legislative Session), and candidate qualifying for that specific process had not yet opened at transcription time — the official qualifying period is August 5-7, 2026 (source: sos.la.gov/elections-voting/election-dates, confirmed 2026-07-15); a nominating-petition path closed July 9, 2026 but produced zero visible qualifiers as of this build. Live-checked the SoS Candidate Inquiry portal's own Nov 3, 2026 election-date view with every office selected: all 6 "U. S. Representative, Nth Congressional District" sections returned "No candidates." `la-official-roster-2026.ts` therefore has NO House entries and is registered in `FIXTURES` for `office: "senate"` only — see that file's docblock for the full reasoning, including why the Feb-2026-dated House filings visible under the portal's OLD closed-primary election-date view (5/16/2026) are stale/superseded by Act 7 and must NOT be used. Louisiana's US SENATE contest (Cassidy's Class 2 seat) IS fully determined already — closed primary (May 16) + runoff (June 27) both already past — and is NOT in scope for this follow-up card.
- OUTCOME: Louisiana's fixture gains a House roster (all 6 districts) built fresh from the post-qualifying official candidate list, registered in `FIXTURES["LA"]` alongside the existing Senate entry.
- IN SCOPE: re-check Louisiana's official source (`voterportal.sos.la.gov/CandidateInquiry?electionDate=20261103`, Statewide/Multi-Parish tab, all 6 House offices selected) after the Aug 5-7, 2026 qualifying period closes; transcribe every qualified House candidate into `la-official-roster-2026.ts`'s (new) `LA_HOUSE_ROSTER_2026` export, cross-checking incumbency against `house.gov`'s member directory (by name, not portal signal — Scalise/LA-1, Carter/LA-2, Higgins/LA-3, Johnson/LA-4, Fields/LA-6 are the current sitting members; LA-5's Letlow is running for Senate instead of re-election, an open seat); register the new House fixture in `FIXTURES["LA"]` in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`; re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch; check whether any race is undetermined pending the Dec 12, 2026 open-general runoff (`runoff_pending` per the epic's standard mechanism) — Louisiana's open-primary format means a Dec 12 runoff is the norm for any seat without a Nov 3 majority winner, not an edge case; also check Louisiana's candidate-withdrawal deadline for the newly-qualified House field per the epic's withdrawal-deadline standing requirement (general LA rule found this build: 4:30pm on the 9th day after the relevant primary date, or 4:30pm 2 days before early voting if exactly 2 candidates remain — La. R.S. 18:1400.1 per usvotefoundation.org, confirmed 2026-07-15; compute the exact date once the House field and any resulting runoff status are known); update `louisiana-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the new candidate-by-candidate comparison; record any NEW governing calendar dates found and open a further `NOT BEFORE` follow-up card if the Dec 12 runoff leaves anything undetermined.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; re-touching the already-determined Senate roster unless a re-check independently surfaces a withdrawal or correction.
- SAFETY: same as the original LA card — official-source reads only, no Ballotpedia as a primary source, never guess an undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently against house.gov (never this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table, and never the Feb-2026 stale closed-primary filings superseded by Act 7).
- NOT BEFORE: 2026-08-08 — the day after Louisiana's Aug 5-7, 2026 US House open-primary qualifying period closes (4:30pm Aug 7); this is the earliest point the SoS Candidate Inquiry portal should show a real, qualifying-closed House candidate list rather than "No candidates" (source: sos.la.gov/elections-voting/election-dates, confirmed 2026-07-15). If the portal's own "UNOFFICIAL until qualifying is closed" banner is still showing on re-check, wait a further day or two for the post-qualifying batch update before transcribing.
- STATUS: Backlog

- STATUS: Backlog

- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b

This re-check is NOT about promoting primary-stage filers — it is about two still-open windows that could still add rows to an otherwise-determined roster: (a) a vacancy-fill independent/political-organization/political-group petition candidate (KRS 118.375(2), deadline August 11, 2026), and (b) a newly-declared write-in candidate (KRS 117.265(2), deadline August 24, 2026), the same date the Secretary of State issues its final certification of candidates' names to county clerks (KRS 118.215(1)(b)) — the true ballot-content lock date for this cycle.
- OUTCOME: KY's fixture is re-verified against the official source after both windows close, confirming no vacancy-fill petition candidate or new write-in was added, or updated to add any that were.
- IN SCOPE: re-verify Kentucky's official source (`web.sos.ky.gov/CandidateFilings/Default.aspx?id=3` for Senate, `id=4` for House) after August 24, 2026, for any new row not present in the original 25-row fixture; also re-check Kentucky's candidate-withdrawal posture (this build found KRS 118.212 sets NO fixed withdrawal deadline — a candidate may withdraw at any point up to the election, per the epic's withdrawal-deadline standing requirement re-check any of the 25 already-filed rows for a withdrawal since 2026-07-15); re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch if any change is found; update `kentucky-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the re-check result either way (a confirmed "no change" is still a required update, not a no-op).
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent.
- SAFETY: same as the original KY card — official-source reads only, no Ballotpedia as a primary source, never guess an undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently (house.gov / senate.gov, never this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table).
- NOT BEFORE: 2026-08-24 — Kentucky Secretary of State's final certification of candidates' names to county clerks (KRS 118.215(1)(b)), the same date as the write-in declaration deadline (KRS 117.265(2)); this is the date after which no further vacancy-fill petition (KRS 118.375(2), its own earlier August 11, 2026 deadline already covered by waiting for this later date) or write-in candidate can be added, making the roster fully locked for the cycle (source: `elect.ky.gov/Resources/Documents/2026%20Election%20Calendar%20Final%20Version%2010_6_2025.pdf`, confirmed 2026-07-15).
- STATUS: Backlog

This re-check is NOT about promoting primary-stage filers — it is about North Carolina's single governing risk window: a general-election nominee's candidate-withdrawal deadline (NCGS §163-114), which NCSBE ties to the date military/overseas absentee ballots begin transmission — **Friday, September 4, 2026** — the same date that functions as this cycle's practical ballot-content lock. A separate, still-unresolved gap (the 2026 general-election write-in candidate filing deadline, not found in the official sources read during the original build) should also be checked at this re-check, since North Carolina does permit write-in candidacies via a separate petition process and no confirmed deadline currently rules one out.

- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-16, opened as part of Utah's own vertical-slice build per the epic's "NOT BEFORE DATE-GATE CONVENTION" standing requirement (every future manual-track build must open its own dated re-check card, not leave it to a retroactive sweep). UT's fixture (`scripts/congressional-rosters/ut-official-roster-2026.ts`, `docs/operations/utah-vertical-slice-data-check.md`) was built at `UT_STAGE = "general"` — Utah's June 23, 2026 primary is fully decided (all 3 contested congressional primaries at 100% locality reporting) and District 4's convention-only nominations are also final, so this is not a primary-to-general promotion re-check. It exists because Utah Code § 20A-9-202(6) permits a candidate to withdraw by filing a written affidavit with the clerk without itself stating a standalone numeric deadline distinct from the ballot-printing timeline — the practical, functional lock point is Utah's own election calendar's September 16, 2026 UOCAVA ballot-transmission deadline (ballots must be finalized/printed by then). Utah's 2026 congressional map is also a genuinely novel case among states built so far — a court-ordered mid-decade redistricting moved 3 of 4 sitting US Representatives (Moore, Maloy, Kennedy) into a different district number than the one they currently hold; this re-check should reconfirm none of the 19 rows changed, not just check for a withdrawal.
- OUTCOME: UT's fixture is re-verified against the official source after the ballot-content lock point, confirming no late withdrawal occurred and no candidate's status changed, or updated to reflect whichever did.
- IN SCOPE: re-verify Utah's official source (`vote.utah.gov/2026-candidate-filings/`) after September 16, 2026, for any status change among the 19 rows (a withdrawal would newly appear as a `Withdrew` status on the filing table); re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch if any change is found; update `utah-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the re-check result either way (a confirmed "no change" is still a required update, not a no-op); also confirm no write-in candidate declared by the August 1, 2026 write-in deadline (per the epic's withdrawal/write-in standing requirement) needs to be added.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent.
- SAFETY: same as the original UT card — official-source reads only, no Ballotpedia as a primary source, never guess an undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently (house.gov, matched by name not old district number given the redistricting, never this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table).

**[P0] NOT BEFORE 2026-08-07: Finalize VA post-primary official roster**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-16, opened as part of Virginia's own vertical-slice build per the epic's "NOT BEFORE DATE-GATE CONVENTION" standing requirement. VA's fixture (`scripts/congressional-rosters/va-official-roster-2026.ts`, `docs/operations/virginia-vertical-slice-data-check.md`) was built at `VA_STAGE = "primary"` and, unlike every prior state's build, is DELIBERATELY PARTIAL: only 2 of 11 US House districts (05, 08) are imported. Virginia splits congressional nomination into two published tracks — contested-primary filers (published now, complete) and "non-primary" automatic nominees for parties with no primary in a district (NOT yet published anywhere on elections.virginia.gov as of build time, despite the Senate/local certification deadline for that track, June 22, 2026, already having passed by ~3.5 weeks). Importing only the known (opposing-party) side for the other 9 districts and the 2026 US Senate race would have made this app's `isIncumbentSeekingReelection` check falsely assert the sitting incumbent isn't seeking re-election — so those 9 districts and the Senate race were omitted entirely instead, per the epic's SAFETY rule. See the data-check doc's "How this was verified" section for the exhaustive (5-path) confirmation that this is a genuine publication gap, not a search gap.
- OUTCOME: VA's fixture covers all 11 US House districts and the 2026 US Senate race (Mark Warner's seat), each row either a determined general-ballot nominee (post-Aug-4-certification) or an accurate primary-stage filer, with no seat's incumbent status left to guess.

If found: add rows for the 9 previously-omitted House districts (01, 02, 03, 04, 06, 07, 09, 10, 11) and the US Senate race (`qualified_for_general_ballot` for determined non-primary nominees, matched against the same house.gov/senate.gov incumbency cross-check this build used) — these seats will not yet reflect the Aug 4 primary outcome (that's a separate step below), so District 5's and District 8's contested-primary rows stay `qualified_for_primary_ballot` in this same pass unless the second step below has also become ready. (2) SEPARATELY, once the SBE certifies the Aug 4 primary (August 18, 2026 — see the "NOT BEFORE" note below for sequencing if this card is picked before that date), promote District 5's and District 8's rows from `qualified_for_primary_ballot` to `qualified_for_general_ballot` for the certified winners (never guess from unofficial election-night numbers); also re-check whether District 8 now has a published Republican nominee. (3) Re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch; update `virginia-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the new candidate-by-candidate comparison for all 11 districts + Senate. (4) Check VA's candidate-withdrawal window and ballot-content certification deadline (both already recorded in the data-check doc) for anything that changed. **[P2] Re-check official roster: South Carolina (SC) — after special Senate primary**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-16, opened as part of South Carolina's own vertical-slice build per the epic's "NOT BEFORE DATE-GATE CONVENTION" standing requirement (every future manual-track build must open its own dated re-check card, not leave it to a retroactive sweep). SC's fixture (`scripts/congressional-rosters/sc-official-roster-2026.ts`, `docs/operations/south-carolina-vertical-slice-data-check.md`) was built at `SC_STAGE = "general"` — South Carolina's June 9, 2026 primary and June 23, 2026 runoff have both already occurred, so all 7 House districts' general-ballot nominees are already determined. The U.S. Senate race is the exception: sitting incumbent Lindsey Graham (R) won his own primary but died before the general election, triggering a brand-new Republican-only special filing period and special primary under S.C. Code § 7-11-55 (confirmed via the SC Election Commission's own press release, `scvotes.gov/u-s-senate-special-republican-party-filing-primary/`) — as of build time, no Republican candidate has even filed yet, so the fixture deliberately omits any Republican Senate row rather than guessing or misusing `runoff_pending` for a contest with no real already-filed finalist.
- OUTCOME: SC's fixture is updated to add the Republican Senate nominee once the S.C. Code § 7-11-55 special process determines one, and re-verified for the House slate's and non-Republican Senate slate's formal certification and any post-certification withdrawal.
- IN SCOPE: re-verify South Carolina's official source (`vrems.scvotes.sc.gov`, Office "U.S. Senate", 2026 General election) after the August 11, 2026 special primary (or August 25, 2026 if a runoff is necessary) for the determined Republican nominee; add that nominee's row to `sc-official-roster-2026.ts` as `qualified_for_general_ballot`, cross-checking incumbency is not applicable (Graham's seat, no incumbent on this special ballot) and independently confirming the nominee via a second official source if one exists; also re-verify the House slate and the already-determined Senate rows are formally CERTIFIED per South Carolina's August 17, 2026 noon deadline (deadline to certify petition/party candidates) — check for any newly-certified independent/petition federal filer not present in this build's VREMS snapshot; also check South Carolina's September 4, 2026, 5:00 p.m. candidate-withdrawal deadline for any already-determined nominee (House or Senate) per the epic's withdrawal-deadline standing requirement; re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch if any change is found; update `south-carolina-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the re-check result either way (a confirmed "no change" is still a required update, not a no-op); record any NEW governing calendar dates found and open a further `NOT BEFORE` follow-up card if anything is still undetermined (e.g. if the August 11 primary requires an August 25 runoff).
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent.
- SAFETY: same as the original SC card — official-source reads only, no Ballotpedia as a primary source, never guess an undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently (house.gov / senate.gov, never this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table).
- NOT BEFORE: 2026-08-12 — the day after South Carolina's August 11, 2026 special Republican Senate primary; scvotes.gov posts election-night results same-day (source: `scvotes.gov/u-s-senate-special-republican-party-filing-primary/`, confirmed 2026-07-16). Note: if that primary requires an August 25, 2026 runoff (no majority winner), hold the final re-run until 2026-08-26 instead. Also note South Carolina's own August 17, 2026 noon certification deadline and September 4, 2026, 5:00 p.m. candidate-withdrawal deadline — if the August 12/26 check finds the Senate nominee determined but either of those later dates hasn't passed yet, a further check after whichever is later may still be warranted before calling the roster fully locked for the cycle.
- STATUS: Backlog
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; SC's fixture reflects the determined Republican Senate nominee (or, if the check finds the August 11 primary required a runoff, this card stays open with an updated `NOT BEFORE: 2026-08-26` instead of being closed); the House slate and non-Republican Senate rows are confirmed certified with no new petition filer or withdrawal; end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on shows correct candidate-by-candidate match; deliverable doc updated with the re-check result and any new governing dates.
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate, same authorized scope as the original SC card and the epic's standing merge-promptly requirement.
- GROOMED: ready: single clear trigger date, sourced directly from the SC Election Commission's own press release and 2026 election calendar, no open questions — 2026-07-16

**[P2] Evaluate the 9 closed Dependabot version bumps before taking any of them**
- SAFE TIER DONE 2026-07-22 (PR #431, merged): the 8 low-risk bumps whose only CI failure was the now-fixed `audit` check — 4 GitHub Actions (`actions/checkout` v6→v7, `actions/setup-node` v6→v7, `actions/upload-artifact` v4→v7.0.1, `dorny/paths-filter` v3→v4.0.2) + 4 npm (`@tailwindcss/postcss` 4.3.3, `@aws-sdk/client-textract` 3.1092.0, `eslint-plugin-prettier` 5.5.6, `@googlemaps/js-api-loader` 2.1.1). Verified via `npm run check` (lint+tsc+3600 tests) + build. NOTE: the "`audit` is pre-existing/flaky, ignore it" guidance below is now VOID — audit went green in PR #430, so it's a real signal for the REMAINING bumps.
- REMAINING (real `test`/`e2e` failures — need investigation, one small branch each): `typescript` 5.9→7.0 (2 majors), `@vitest/coverage-v8` 3→4 (major), `prettier` 3.5.3→3.9.6 (formatting churn), `jscpd` 4→5 (major, dev), `eslint-config-next` 15→16 (major), `react` + `@types/react`, `react-dom` 19.1→19.2.8, `eslint` 9→10 (major), `@vitejs/plugin-react` 5→6 (major).
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-21 — 9 Dependabot PRs sat with red CI for days with nobody actually evaluating whether the bump was safe, worth fixing, or worth skipping; closed unevaluated rather than let them keep rotting silently (a closed, unreviewed PR is worse than no PR — it looks tracked without being acted on). Queuing the evaluation here instead of relying on Dependabot's own PRs as the tracking mechanism.
- TASK: for each package below, on its own small branch — bump to the target version, run `npm run check` (lint + tsc + full test suite), skim the release notes for the version range, then decide: (a) safe -> open a PR and merge; (b) breaking but a quick fix -> fix it in the same PR; (c) not worth it right now -> leave it (Dependabot will re-propose later) or add an `ignore:` rule in `.github/dependabot.yml` if this major version specifically isn't worth chasing.
- Queued bumps (closed PR # for reference; all failed `test`/`e2e` as of 2026-07-21 except where noted):
  - `typescript` 5.9.3 -> 7.0.2 (was #402) — 2 major versions
  - `@vitest/coverage-v8` 3.2.1 -> 4.1.10 (was #403) — major version
  - `prettier` 3.5.3 -> 3.9.6 (was #404) — looked like a minor bump but still failed test/e2e, worth checking why
  - `jscpd` 4.0.5 -> 5.0.12 (was #407) — major version, devDependency (duplication scanner only)
  - `eslint-config-next` 15.2.4 -> 16.2.11 (was #408) — major version
  - `actions/checkout` 6 -> 7 (was #397) — GitHub Action; all real checks passed once the parity-gate crash (fixed #412) stopped blocking it
  - `actions/setup-node` 6 -> 7 (was #396) — same, all real checks passed
  - `actions/upload-artifact` 4.6.2 -> 7.0.1 (was #398) — same, all real checks passed
  - `dorny/paths-filter` 3.0.3 -> 4.0.2 (was #395) — same, all real checks passed
- NOTE: the 4 GitHub Actions bumps are likely low-effort to re-verify and take — they were CI-clean except for the now-fixed parity-gate self-reference crash. The 5 npm/yarn ones have genuine test/e2e failures needing real investigation.
- SECOND BATCH — closed unevaluated 2026-07-21 to avoid a 10-PR merge-queue pileup (Muxin's call). Same evaluate-then-decide task applies. CI state captured at close (`audit` fails on ALL ten — it's a pre-existing/flaky `npm audit` job, NOT the bump, so ignore it as signal; judge on `test`/`e2e`):
  - LIKELY SAFE — real checks (`test`+`e2e`) GREEN, only `audit` red:
    - `@tailwindcss/postcss` 4.2.1 -> 4.3.3 (was #415) — minor
    - `@aws-sdk/client-textract` 3.1055.0 -> 3.1092.0 (was #416) — patch
    - `eslint-plugin-prettier` 5.4.1 -> 5.5.6 (was #418) — minor, devDependency
    - `@googlemaps/js-api-loader` 2.0.2 -> 2.1.1 (was #424) — minor
  - REAL FAILURES — `test` (and/or `e2e`) red, need investigation:
    - `prettier` 3.5.3 -> 3.9.6 (was #422) — `test` red (formatting churn likely; same package as first-batch #404)
    - `eslint-config-next` 15.2.4 -> 16.2.11 (was #423) — `test` red, major (dup of first-batch #408)
    - `react` + `@types/react` (was #417) — `test`+`e2e` red

    - `react-dom` 19.1.0 -> 19.2.8 (was #420) — `test`+`e2e` red
    - `@vitejs/plugin-react` 5.1.4 -> 6.0.3 (was #421) — `test`+`e2e` red, major
- RESOLVED 2026-07-22 — all 9 REMAINING bumps evaluated on their own branches (bump → `npm run check` + build, e2e where the failure was runtime, release-note read). Each verified locally, then PR'd with auto-merge-after-self-vet:
  - MERGE (took):
    - `react` + `react-dom` 19.1.0→19.2.8 — PR #434 (MERGED). Minor; check+build+e2e (39 passed) green.
    - `jscpd` 4.0.5→5.0.12 (dev) — PR #435. jscpd 5's detector finds intra-file clones the v4 baseline lacked → regenerated `scripts/quality/duplication-baseline.json` (zero source changes; report shape unchanged). check+build+dup:check green.
    - `prettier` 3.5.3→3.9.6 — PR #433. The real churn was 26 lint-gated `src` files (3.9 collapses short union types onto one line); also hardened `.prettierignore` to keep `npm run format` out of vendored `design-handoff/` + harness `.claude/` (else ~47k lines of churn). NOTE: trips the security-review-label gate because prettier cosmetically reformats 3 `src/app/api/**` route files — diff is whitespace/union-syntax only; `security-reviewed` label applied 2026-07-22 to unblock.
  - DEFER (`.github/dependabot.yml` ignore rules, all in PR #436 — minor/patch incl. security still flow):
    - `eslint` 9→10 + `eslint-config-next` 15→16 (majors): coupled to a **Next 16** upgrade. config-next 15 peers `eslint ^7||^8||^9`; eslint 10 + config-next 15 hard-fails `next lint` (bundled `@rushstack/eslint-patch` rejects eslint 10); config-next 16 requires Next 16; `next lint` itself is removed in Next 16.
    - `typescript` 7 (>=7.0.0 ignored): **incompatible with Next 15.5**. TS 7 (native compiler) drops the classic `ts.sys`; Next 15.5's `next.config.ts` loader calls `ts.findConfigFile(cwd, ts.sys.fileExists, …)` → crashes, breaking `next lint` AND `next build`. Not patchable here; no stable TS 6.x exists (npm latest = 7.0.2). Aside from that, TS 7 type-checked the repo with one trivial `declare module "*.css"` fix — the blocker is purely the Next integration. Revisit with Next 16.
    - `vitest` 4 + `@vitest/coverage-v8` 4 + `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6 (majors): dev-only test tooling, no runtime/product benefit (Next builds with turbopack, not vite; `npm audit --omit=dev` already scopes the audit gate). Real migration: vitest 4 forbids `new` on arrow-fn mock impls (breaks AWS/Anthropic SDK mocks) + changed async module-resolution timing (breaks the `next/dynamic` stub in `page.test.tsx`); plugin-react 6 forces vite 8 + a full lock regen. Fixes are known/small; the cost is the vite-8 churn. Revisit deliberately.
- FOLLOW-UP (separate, optional): a `depcheck`-style pass for genuinely-dead dependencies (imported nowhere) — distinct from these bumps. DONE 2026-07-23 (PR #448): removed `pixelmatch` (only fed the retired parity-gate) + `@dnd-kit/{core,sortable,utilities}` (consumer `IssueRanker.tsx` deleted); 0 refs repo-wide; check+build green. depcheck's other flags (jscpd/tailwind/stryker/eslint*/lhci/coverage-v8) are CLI/config false positives, left intact.
- STATUS: Done

**[P3] Skip the Stryker mutation gate for formatting-only changes to scoped files**
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-23 — the prettier 3.9 bump (PR #433) triggered a **60+ min** full Stryker run and had to be admin-merged past the still-pending `mutation` gate (everything else green). Root cause: `.github/workflows/mutation.yml` already gates Stryker behind a `dorny/paths-filter` scoped-path list AND already skips all `dependabot[bot]` PRs — that's why non-scoped PRs (e.g. jscpd #435) pass in ~9s. But prettier reformatted `src/lib/server/budget.ts`, which IS on the scoped list, so the filter matched and full Stryker ran — even though the change was whitespace/union-syntax only (identical AST → no mutant outcome can possibly change). Pure wasted compute + a merge stall.
- CHECK FIRST (Muxin's flag): simple-kanban may already carry this exact workflow fix — its mutation/CI workflow is the shared-pattern ancestor referenced in `mutation.yml`'s header comment. Diff simple-kanban's `mutation.yml` (and its maintenance-loops docs) BEFORE building; port their approach if it exists rather than inventing a parallel one.
- TASK (only if not already solved upstream): in the "Decide whether to run Stryker" step, when the PR's diff to the matched scoped files is formatting-only, skip Stryker (report the gate passed, same as the existing non-scoped skip path). Candidate approaches: (a) re-run `prettier --check` / a whitespace-insensitive `git diff -w --quiet` over just the scoped files that matched and skip if clean; (b) gate on whether `next lint`/prettier would reformat. Keep the existing "human editing a genuinely scoped file still gets full coverage" guarantee — only formatting-equivalent diffs skip.
- WHY P3: rare trigger (only a formatting PR that happens to touch a scoped high-stakes file), and admin-merge is a clean one-off escape hatch, so low urgency. Worth doing because prettier-scale reformats recur and a 60-min pointless gate is a real stall each time.
- STATUS: Backlog

**[P2] Evaluate removing the parity-gate check entirely (is it earning its keep?)**
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-21 — Muxin: increasingly skeptical the parity-gate is doing us any favors. As a *required* status check on `main` it now blocks legitimate merges whenever copy or visuals intentionally change — which is most design work — and the references go stale the moment we ship a redesign, so the gate flags the intended change as a failure. Two concrete instances today (both had ALL real checks — `test`/`e2e`/`mutation`/`check`/`review-gallery` — green, blocked only by parity-gate, and were admin-merged past it):
  - #413 fix(copy): privacy "never stored" trust copy — parity-gate red purely because the hero label + CTA subtext strings changed vs the reference screenshots.
  - #414 feat(repcard): approved money-section redesign — parity-gate red because the redesign *intentionally* changes the card visuals; the reference is the OLD design.
- CONTEXT / prior known issues (see memory `parity_gate_trust_model`, `keystone_gap_reassessment`): the gate has a documented history of a crop false-FAIL (fixed #261), a visual-only false-PASS on ~11 scenarios (passes different-but-similar pages), and a self-reference crash (fixed #412). The "flip to required" happened at some point — it is now in `required_status_checks` on `main` alongside `test`/`e2e`/`mutation`.
- TASK: decide the gate's fate. Options to weigh:
  - (a) REMOVE entirely — drop `parity-gate` from branch-protection required checks + delete/retire the workflow + `scripts/design/parity-gate.ts` and its scenario/reference machinery.
  - (b) DEMOTE to non-required (advisory) — keep it running for signal but stop it blocking merges; revisit references opportunistically.
  - (c) KEEP but fix the workflow so references auto-regenerate as part of an intentional-design PR (so an approved visual change updates its own baseline instead of failing).
- DECISION NEEDED (Muxin): which of (a)/(b)/(c). Leaning toward remove/demote given how often it false-blocks real design work.
- OBSERVATIONS (2026-07-22, whiteboard-v4 session — third and fourth instances, plus new evidence):
  - #427 (whiteboard v4, 11-frame redesign): every real check green (`test`/`e2e`/`mutation`/`check`/`review-gallery`), parity-gate red at **4/28 scenarios passing** — the refs are now one full design generation behind (Keystone canvas vs the whiteboard). Admin-merged.
  - #428 (a 2-file tooling fix, no visual change at all): blocked identically, because the gate is red on **main itself** — every `design-parity` run since at least 07-21 fails, on pushes and PRs alike. A check that fails on everything discriminates nothing; it can no longer tell a regression from an intentional change from a no-op.
  - Operational cost is worse than "annoying": the Claude Code auto-mode classifier (correctly) refuses `gh pr merge --admin` from agents, so a required-but-always-red check converts every merge into a manual Muxin action — it broke unattended pipeline merging entirely today.
  - Counter-evidence worth keeping: the *screenshot artifacts* were genuinely valuable this session — visually auditing the regenerated review-gallery caught 5 real integration bugs the unit/e2e suites missed. All of that signal came from humans/agents LOOKING at the screenshots; none of it came from the pixel-diff pass/fail verdict.
- RECOMMENDATIONS (2026-07-22):
  1. IMMEDIATE, regardless of final ruling: **demote to non-required** (option b) — a one-line branch-protection change that unblocks merging today while keeping the workflow running for signal.
  2. THEN pick the end state: option (a) retire the pixel-diff *verdict* but keep `review-gallery` as the committed human-review artifact (where today's real value was), or option (c) only if it includes **in-PR rebaselining** (an approved intentional-change PR regenerates its own refs + structural probes, so the baseline can never go a design-generation stale again). Plain (c) without auto-rebaseline just re-creates today's situation after the next redesign.
  3. If any gate stays, fix the ~11-scenario visual-only false-PASS first (the known trust bug) — a gate that both false-fails intentional work and false-passes similar-but-wrong pages is negative signal.
- DECISION (2026-07-22, Muxin): option (a) — RETIRE the pixel-diff verdict entirely, KEEP the screenshot gallery. NO metered API gate (a VLM CI judge was built and then dropped — Muxin would rather not pay a dedicated API cost per PR). The "did the design we asked for get built?" check instead rides on the pipeline's existing self-vet agent, which already reads the review-gallery screenshots (where all this session's real value came from). Regression stays with e2e.
- SHIPPED (2026-07-22, branch `chore/retire-parity-gate-add-design-judge`): deleted `scripts/design/parity-gate.ts` + `gate-verdict.ts` + `gate-summary.ts` and their npm scripts; rewrote `design-parity.yml` → "Design Review" (review-gallery standalone, posts the before/after PR comment; no parity-gate job, no judge job); `docs/maintenance.md` required-checks line now `test`/`e2e`/`mutation`. Muxin removed `parity-gate` from `main` branch-protection required checks. → move to Done once the branch merges.
- STATUS: Backlog

**[P2] Fix the red `audit` CI check (npm audit high: sharp, via next)**
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-22 — the `audit` job (`dependency-audit.yml`, `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`) has been red on main since at least 2026-07-21. The Dependabot-evaluation card above dismissed it as "pre-existing/flaky — ignore as signal", but it is NOT flaky: it fails deterministically on two upstream advisories, both pulled in transitively by `next`:
  - `sharp` <0.35.0 — HIGH (this is what trips `--audit-level=high`)
  - `postcss` <8.5.10 — moderate (GHSA-qx2v-qp2m-jg93, XSS via unescaped `</style>`)
- Why it matters even though `audit` is not a required check: a permanently-red advisory check trains everyone to ignore audit output entirely (already happening — see the note above), which is exactly when a real vulnerable-dependency alert gets missed.
- TASK (pick one, smallest that goes green):
  - (a) Bump `next` to a release that depends on `sharp` >=0.35.0 / patched `postcss` (check whether one exists yet; may ride along with the queued `eslint-config-next` major evaluation).
  - (b) If no such `next` release exists, add npm `overrides` for `sharp`/`postcss` in package.json, verify `npm run check` + a prod build still pass (sharp is Next's image pipeline — smoke-test image rendering).
  - (c) Last resort: scope the advisory out of the audit command with a documented justification + expiry note, so the check goes back to meaning something.
- Cross-ref: the "[P2] Evaluate the 9 closed Dependabot version bumps" card — whoever does that evaluation should fold this in, since every one of those PRs showed the same red `audit`.
- RESOLVED 2026-07-22 (PR #430, merged): combined (a)+(b) — `next` 15.5.20 → 15.5.21 (patch; also cleared 8 high-severity Next.js CVEs that were in the same report) + `overrides` `sharp ^0.35.0` (→0.35.3) and `postcss ^8.5.10` (→8.5.22, dedup'd across next/tailwind/vite). `npm audit --omit=dev` now = 0 vulnerabilities; typecheck + prod build verified. The "ignore audit as flaky" assumption on the Dependabot card above is now VOID — audit is a true signal again.
- STATUS: Done
